Invalid URL/coercion fallbacks return the original configured URL. These values remain checkout metadata, not proof of payment or entitlement.

Webhook verification preserves exact legacy boundaries: absent secrets permit sandbox requests; Stripe handles missing headers as false, while Lemon accesses headers before its catch. Stripe converts raw bytes to UTF-8, parses the existing timestamp form, checks the same absolute replay tolerance and accepts any matching v1 candidate. Lemon hashes original raw bytes. JSON fallback, malformed lengths, serialization/crypto errors, logging and exceptions retain preceding behavior. Type-only assertions at the crypto/coercion boundaries preserve native validation instead of adding conversions or changing accepted inputs.

createDatabaseClient extracts shared SDK initialization from index.js into a typed structural factory port. The index continues importing the installed Supabase SDK and exposing the same client. URL/secret defaults are captured once; auth session persistence and automatic refresh stay disabled. Each request resolves the current ambient lease and global transport, preserves request options, and combines request/lease cancellation with AbortSignal.timeout(15000). Transport errors propagate without retry. All index content from saveReminder through its final domain reexports is byte-for-byte unchanged, preserving reminder persistence and background worker behavior. This increment does not migrate those remaining function bodies or change SQL, SDK configuration or authorization.

## Phase 5 increment: Mercado Pago and remaining database-index persistence in TypeScript

createMercadoPagoClient migrates the seven public plan/subscription/signature APIs into strict TypeScript with environment, fetch and logger ports. The host client.js is now a stable hoisted facade; client.d.ts derives signatures. Test-phone and explicit-plan routing still precede cached plans/token lookup. Search keeps exact normalized active matches before broad Hermes/Reminder/Pro matches, the same search-failure null behavior and original search/create/cache order. Plan and pending-subscription payloads retain monthly recurrence, defaults, live token lookup, callback construction, user external reference and fallback payer email. GET/cancel preserve complete provider response objects; plan/create preserve original selected fields. HTTP errors, native JSON/transport exceptions and logging retain preceding semantics. Cancellation sends canceled only after a non-OK cancelled response and never introduces replay after transport/parse exceptions. The plan cache remains per process/client; it is not a new single-flight or distributed idempotency guarantee.

All 25 remaining index functions plus the cancellation alias now execute through typed modules: reminder-persistence handles creation/deduplication, acknowledgement, mutation/cancellation and calendar/task companions; reminder-delivery-persistence handles nudge/due reads, delivery status and claim/peek operations; reminder-overview handles follow-up lifecycle, local agenda and recent reminder queries. reminder-persistence-client defines the consumed row/query/clock/lease/helper ports. index.js retains the actual shared SDK client, helper assembly, hoisted compatibility exports and byte-identical domain reexports. index.d.ts derives reminder signatures and explicitly lists public domain exports, avoiding conflicting private Store aliases from transitional declarations. Factories do not access storage on construction. Ambient lease assertions, user/phone predicates, prefix lookup order, schema-cache fallback, exact errors, companion dates/counts and aliases remain intact.

One deliberate correction accompanies migration: getDailyOverview previously executed an unfiltered tasks query when only phoneNumber was supplied. The typed implementation now performs only the phone-scoped reminder read in that branch; task collections remain empty until userId is present. User-based requests still filter both reminder and task queries by user_id. No new lookup, broader query or data exposure is introduced. Four direct intentional-difference comparisons, typed query-port and actual SDK tests, and the production smoke validate the guard. No other intentional behavior change is introduced. Existing optimistic acknowledgement/nudge observations and legacy error fallbacks remain observations, not transactional proof.

Scheduler delivery behavior remains unchanged: the same reminder projections, nudge count/interval boundaries, RPC-first claim, bounded ten-candidate conditional fallback, pending/expired-processing predicates, lease timestamps and next-pending query are preserved. Worker code, SQL and locking functions are untouched. Real SDK serialization and existing scheduler regression tests verify the migrated adapter boundary; this is not a new SQL/concurrency proof. Mercado Pago webhook validation keeps original manifest ID precedence, header handling, raw-body fallback, constant-time comparison and native exception boundaries. No replay policy or missing-secret configuration redesign is included.

## Phase 5 increment: typed Calendar HTTP webhook and watch-renewal job

createGoogleCalendarWebhook and createCalendarWatchRenewal move the Calendar push handler and watch-renewal job into strict TypeScript with consumed integration/event, reconciliation, clock, logger and timer ports in watch-ports.ts. Their JS entrypoints now assemble original host adapters and retain hoisted public exports; derived declarations preserve public call signatures. Actual Node/Express headers are accepted through an opaque header record with type-only consumed-field assertions. This adds no schema/authentication policy. Construction performs no integration lookup, watch API call or timer startup.

The HTTP handler keeps missing-channel rejection, immediate 200 ACK before integration lookup, original token/channel routing, latest metadata reread under the user queue, obsolete-channel stop, silent baseline and incremental-sync behavior. Changed and cancelled event IDs retain their original filtering, full-sync recovery suppresses discovery, and new token persistence follows successful reconciliation. Remote failures retain existing logging after the ACK. The job keeps silent baseline creation, the 24-hour renewal threshold, old-channel stop before setup, nonfatal stop failures, per-integration error counts, explicit startup with an initial unawaited pass and the six-hour periodic timer. Google SDK, reconciliation, database persistence and reminder scheduler implementations are unchanged. Reviewed official [push notification](https://developers.google.com/workspace/calendar/api/guides/push) and [incremental sync](https://developers.google.com/workspace/calendar/api/guides/sync) guidance; no API or transport setting changes are introduced.

One deliberate correction fixes retained idle queue entries. The preceding handler stored a chained promise but compared the unchained current promise during deletion, so completed users stayed in the map. createUserWebhookQueue compares the actual stored tail, deleting only when no successor replaced it. Same-user serialization, independent-user progress, error propagation and successor recovery remain intact. Tests cover queued successors, rejected tasks and 100 completed users; a direct preceding-source comparison observes one retained old entry versus zero new entries. This remains a process-local queue, not distributed webhook/renewal coordination or durable delivery proof.

## Phase 5 increment: Stripe-only billing, typed callback/dispatch and background memory

Stripe is now the sole active billing provider, following the user's explicit instruction to remove both Lemon Squeezy and Mercado Pago. Their clients, callbacks, compiler roots, server routes, credential examples and provider-specific tests are removed, as is the obsolete Mercado Pago live E2E script. Generated retired-provider modules are cleaned. Checkout selection always returns Stripe, regardless of phone country or stale/unknown BILLING_PROVIDER settings. Subscription info and prompt grounding identify Stripe; legal copy and WhatsApp upgrade cards use its secure-link presentation. Dead provider-name/price fallbacks are removed, including absent commercial metadata. Old conversation payment URLs remain informational through generic checkout/buy/pay/subscription path detection; no retired provider is reactivated. The AI model gateway remains agnostic and unchanged.

Historical subscription data, the existing mp_preapproval_id column and compatibility persistence functions are retained; this increment executes no data/schema migration. schema.sql changes only a descriptive comment, with unchanged DDL verified against its preceding source. Historical migration evidence remains a record of the work at that time; this section supersedes its multi-provider runtime guidance. No existing remote subscription/account is changed by code retirement.

createStripeWebhook moves the callback into strict TypeScript with billing-webhook-ports for the consumed request/response, query, clock and sender boundaries. The JS handler retains hoisted assembly/delegation and a derived declaration. Signed-event handling, early ACK, user lookup order, subscription/status/date payloads and bilingual welcome text remain. Two safety fixes are explicit: returned SDK mutation errors stop welcome delivery; uncertain/unclassified text failures no longer trigger another message format. Only an owned KapsoValidationError with an explicit 400/404/422 rejection allows one template fallback. A real Supabase SDK synthetic transport test verifies emitted user/subscription filters and an insert rejection that suppresses welcome. No event-ID deduplication, durable webhook queue or transactional payment receipt is introduced.

KapsoError and its five subclasses plus createKapsoDispatch now execute in strict TypeScript. kapso-client.js retains shared class identity, lazy host capabilities and the remaining payload builders/transport methods. The dispatch kernel preserves attempt/accepted checkpoints, lease checks, current lease plus 15-second timeout, provider message identity admission, processed-message marking, exact error classification and the existing bounded 429 waits (1000/2000/4000 ms). Network, 5xx, malformed/missing receipts remain uncertain and are never replayed. Existing explicit 400/404/422 payload-format fallback remains. The typed sender return retains the original full provider data. The remaining WhatsApp ingress, payload builders, typing/media methods and jobs are not yet full TypeScript.

createBackgroundMemoryExtraction moves the memory job into a strict injected queue, preserving eligibility filtering, original input/explicit-undefined fields, dispositions/proposals, serial execution and failed-job logging without replay. Embedding scope is captured at enqueue for each owner. A test with the actual embedding service schedules two owners through another context and observes one cached vector request per owner, proving isolation. The JS adapter resolves memory service functions lazily when used: initial broad verification caught eager constructor reads breaking partial mocks/initialization graphs; the final implementation restores that boundary without expanding those mocks. This captures embedding context only and adds no operational turn ownership or durable job guarantee.

Stripe cancellation now uses createStripeSubscriptionClient and the original shared subscription store. Following the official [cancellation](https://docs.stripe.com/api/subscriptions/cancel) and [authentication](https://docs.stripe.com/api/authentication) contracts, it makes one fenced DELETE to the fixed Stripe subscription endpoint with a server-only STRIPE_SECRET_KEY, composes the timeout/turn signal, and requires the exact subscription ID with status canceled before returning. Missing configuration, unsupported legacy IDs, transport/parse/HTTP errors or unconfirmed responses stop local cancellation. The domain tool reports that cancellation could not be confirmed instead of success. No remote failure is downgraded to a local success, no automatic retry is introduced, and no actual remote cancellation occurs during verification. A confirmed remote result followed by local failure remains a cross-service uncertainty, not atomic proof. STRIPE_SECRET_KEY must be configured before enabling real cancellation; the public Payment Link and existing webhook secret remain separate settings.

## Connected canary admission, durable V2 composition and single-delivery transport

The public router now reads AGENT_CORE_V2 for each new admission. Only the exact value true enables selection, and an authenticated user ID must exactly match config/agent-core-v2-canary.json targeting.userAllowlist. AGENT_CORE_V2_ALLOWLIST, when present, is a JSON array replacement; [] clears admission. Invalid/missing configuration, invalid overrides, nonzero percentage targeting and other switch values fail closed to baseline. The default remains false with zero percent and an empty allowlist. Percentage rollout stages in the manifest remain future plans, not an implemented hash-based cohort.

Queue-backed turns perform the existing bounded, lease-fenced prior-turn read, including a search over all older unknown turns. A V2 genesis or verified completed V2 checkpoint can enter the durable ProductivityTurnRunner composition; prior baseline conversations remain pinned to baseline until an explicit authenticated handoff exists. Unknown, corrupt, mixed or already-started current V2 state blocks either engine. Disabling the switch or removing an allowlist entry routes the next completed-boundary turn through baseline without clearing/replaying checkpoints, receipts, tools or deliveries. It does not cancel a running turn or resume the same admitted lease in baseline. CLI/background calls without a complete inbound lease retain baseline. Unsupported media stays baseline before admission; unsupported input on a selected existing V2 conversation is blocked rather than silently transferring engines.

The V2 composition uses the configured model gateway, typed task/reminder/Calendar registry, persisted execution journal and canonical WhatsApp presenter. Canary membership is routing authority, not write authorization: mutations still require an explicit trusted per-turn agentCoreAuthorizeWrite policy, and destructive delete capability is not granted. The current webhook supplies recipient-bound text/CTA/flow delivery but no write policy, so the initial webhook canary is read-only. Unit tests exercise a specifically host-authorized task completion and prove that the same model decision cannot mutate without that policy. Live mutation admission remains a separate host-policy gate.

Durable V2 owns its send/checkpoint lifecycle. The webhook recognizes accepted message IDs through an in-process WeakMap keyed by the returned object, sends no second reply, persists the real provider ID, and skips baseline background memory extraction for those V2 replies. Model/user JSON cannot forge this proof. Unconfirmed V2 delivery remains fenced unknown with no transport or baseline retry. Ordinary baseline send priority and memory behavior are preserved.

The four workspace packages are now production dependencies at their existing versions. Docker ships their compiled dist and package.json through the production workspace links, together with the canary manifest; no source/compiler/tsx is added to the runner. The compiled runtime graph gate now traverses literal dynamic imports, so lazy V2 imports cannot hide development dependencies or missing emitted modules. A separate five-labs-reminder-api:canary-selector candidate image is built and verified without retagging the previously certified production image.

Verification and exact candidate image ID: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/canary-router-admission.json. No remote variable change, live database/provider/message call, deployment or V2 activation is performed. External persistence/transport acceptance, live provider quality/cost/latency, existing-conversation handoff and host write-policy admission remain open. Rollback keeps AGENT_CORE_V2=false and the allowlist empty; retain durable receipts/checkpoints and quarantine uncertain turns. A code rollback restores only this increment's router/webhook/runtime-graph/packaging/configuration changes and removes its new modules/tests/documentation/evidence; any persisted V2 turn requires the same guarded completed-boundary handoff, never a blind replay.


## V2 webhook write authorization and full eligible-user admission

The WhatsApp webhook now supplies agentCoreAuthorizeWrite to routeMessage. It captures the authenticated routed user ID, checks the scoped abort signal before and after assertTurnLease, and approves only a matching TurnContext.userId. Lease failure, cancellation or a foreign owner prevents approval. The existing V2 runner still checks observed target identity, scoped registry capabilities and the durable journal; the flag does not add destructive delete capability. The existing domain implementations retain their own ownership/subscription checks.

The selector now supports manifest percentage 0 or 100, exact user IDs and the wildcard *. With AGENT_CORE_V2=true, percentage 100 or a wildcard admits every authenticated eligible user. The manifest is prepared at percentage 100 with the environment switch still false. AGENT_CORE_V2_ALLOWLIST is an explicit replacement of both manifest allowlist and global percentage: [] clears all admission, a selective list narrows it, and ["*"] enables global selection. Percentages other than 0/100 remain unsupported and fail closed. No environment or production traffic flag is changed externally.

Full selection does not bypass durable admission: existing baseline conversations stay pinned until an authenticated handoff exists, unsupported media retains the prior admission rules, and started/unknown/corrupt V2 turns remain blocked. Rollback still operates at the next completed turn boundary without re-executing effects or transferring a running lease.

Eight added offline cases cover global percentage/wildcard selection and routing, anonymous rejection, dynamic rollback, environment override precedence, webhook owner/lease/cancellation authorization and the complete webhook → global selector → actual durable V2 runner → registered task adapter path. That last case confirms one journalled task completion, one accepted WhatsApp send and persistence of its provider message ID using injected provider/domain/storage ports. The original worker and webhook cases reuse the same extracted fixture and retain their assertions. Existing missing-host-policy and destructive-capability rejection checks remain.

Complete verification and candidate image certification: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-write-authorization-global-rollout.json. No live database, model, WhatsApp, billing, remote variable or deployment action is performed. Live provider and external persistence/transport gates remain open. Code rollback restores only this increment's selector/webhook/port/configuration/example and test edits, removes the shared fixture and appended documentation/evidence, and rebuilds runtime; the prior guarded canary remains intact. Disabling AGENT_CORE_V2 preserves checkpoints/receipts and quarantines uncertain turns.


## Live routing and token diagnostic: global selection does not migrate baseline conversations

A read-only production inspection after deployment 523b104 confirmed AGENT_CORE_V2=true with no environment allowlist override. Four recent completed queue turns have no V2 checkpoint and correlate with baseline strict foreground request traces; one later completed turn has an agentCoreProductivity checkpoint. The public router's prior && !priorV2 condition deliberately retains existing baseline conversations. Global percentage100 therefore enables eligible genesis/V2 continuation but does not complete existing-conversation handoff. Infrastructure health and a true flag do not prove that an existing user's turn entered V2.

The official OpenRouter generation metadata API confirmed native prompt counts of 8121 and 8117 on Groq and 8912 on Cerebras. Runtime request traces record Groq's provider error followed by the configured strict baseline Cerebras fallback. Those requests sent 33 messages and a 17453-byte structured schema; the router projected 30 prior history messages. Byte counts are not provider tokenizer measurements. The failed Groq generation returned404 from the metadata endpoint, so its failure is established by the separate runtime trace rather than fabricated generation statistics.

The V2 composition also does not configure the planned1800-token target. It inherits AgentLoop's32000 input/total default and a conservative serialized-byte reservation instead of a provider-specific tokenizer. It returns no usage/attempt metadata here, and typed AIResponse has no generation/provider identity. The diagnosed IDs come from baseline traces; this inspection cannot report measured tokens for the sampled V2 turn. The proposed token reduction and existing-user engine migration remain incomplete despite successful TypeScript/build/unit/container checks.

Required next work is authenticated completed-baseline handoff preserving references/receipts/active state, a provider-aware whole-request budget including tool/schema/framing overhead, and per-turn/per-attempt V2 engine/provider/generation/native-usage observability. Removing the pin or resetting persisted conversation/checkpoints would bypass continuity safeguards and is not a migration implementation. No production variables, database rows, model calls or WhatsApp messages were changed/generated during diagnosis. Bounded sample and provenance: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/production-routing-token-diagnosis.json.

## V2 transcribed audio, terminal admission rejection and productivity mutation parity

Transcribed audio now uses the same leased V2 path as text when normalized text is present and attachmentOnly is false. An unsupported turn in a completed V2 conversation is rejected before engine dispatch with a durable, owner-bound agentCoreAdmissionRejected certificate. The worker recognizes the private host error object and closes processing as a terminal rejection rather than an uncertain action. An arbitrary exception with the same code does not qualify. Actual uncertain writes, deliveries, expired leases and previously started current runs retain their reconciliation fences.

The thread reader preserves the last actual conversation while skipping those certificates. For the legacy audio-admission bug it also recognizes the existing backend producer's PRIOR_TURN_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED gate error with an unknown status and a null or empty response: the old router emitted that gate before either engine, and V2 always published its execution namespace before domain dispatch. This rule does not apply to generic empty unknown rows or any execution/delivery checkpoint. Both the unknown scan and latest-conversation lookup remain thread-scoped and lease-fenced, inspect older unsafe unknowns, and enforce bounded monotonic cursors. No row is reset, requeued, replayed or replaced with invented conversation history. Production recovery depends on the exact stored error/response evidence matching this rule after deployment.

V2 now registers reminder_cancel and enables task_delete, calendar_update and calendar_delete. Cancellation changes reminder status to cancelled using the existing logical-routine and linked-notice implementation, preserves history, and reports confirmed IDs, partial results and already-inactive outcomes. Cancellation children retain their advance-notice identity for presentation. Destructive operations require the named capability, an observed resource, the existing host authorization callback and a durable journal. Calendar mutations verify the target through an authenticated original-date read, pass that date through legacy deletion resolution, validate returned identity, support timed/all-day update fields, and quarantine unconfirmed companion outcomes. These additions do not change the delivery scheduler.

The model receives a compact projection of tool observations and relevant references; complete records and receipts remain in durable history/checkpoints. IDs already represented in the retained recent observations are not repeated in system references. Reads reserve 256 estimated input tokens for continuation within the 1800 per-call ceiling. A successful scoped read can expose its small cancellation/deletion followup without a second catalogue-selection call; after a write, the active schema follows that operation instead of retaining the larger read schema. Whole-request wire guards and the 12000 cumulative turn ceiling remain enforced. Dates still pass the original backend validation. This is an estimated input target across model-specific tokenizers; reported provider usage remains authoritative.

Confirmed mutation cards supersede intermediate read cards only when every displayed resource has a complete confirmed mutation. Partial cancellation never claims that the entire routine was cancelled. The compiled Node 22 offline smoke exercises transcribed-audio admission, both null and empty legacy gate/cascade responses, owner-bound baseline handoff, reminder_read followed by reminder_cancel, one synthetic status update and one accepted synthetic delivery, with no notices. Its four wire estimates are 820, 1093, 1405 and 1436 tokens. There are two domain steps and four model requests including discovery and final completion; reported usage in this smoke is synthetic.

Verification: 3707 unit tests in 292 files, strict typecheck without errors, source-migration-inventory --check with 723 TypeScript sources and zero maintained JavaScript files, successful Docker build, original compiled server startup in Node 22.23.3, health 200 and graceful SIGTERM exit 0. Evidence: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-audio-productivity-parity.json. No Railway deployment, live database modification, paid model request or live WhatsApp delivery was performed. Deploy the certified change before evaluating production recovery; uncertain effect checkpoints must not be cleared to force admission.

### Production continuation regression (2026-10-14)

Production turn c25d61ca-cfd7-4ae0-95c0-6eb7c729d852 admitted transcribed audio and completed reminder_read, but made only two model requests and reached its context limit before reminder_cancel. The previous local smoke had an empty conversation. A new regression includes prior dialogue and two reminders, measures the native OpenRouter request envelope, and reproduces a read without the subsequent mutation before the fix.

Bounded productivity now prefers the longest fitting dialogue window, but may summarize earlier turns down to the complete active user turn instead of requiring three full recent turns. Operational summaries preserve unresolved approvals, clarifications and unknown effects; current assistant/tool pairs, owned resource references, durable conversation and write authorization remain intact. Reference projection excludes only IDs visible in the active turn, so an earlier observation removed by compaction does not remove the system reference needed for a follow-up. Default reminder/productivity callers retain their three-turn floor. Oversized essential current observations or pending state still stop safely; no universal completion guarantee is implied by the input target.

The memory-extraction skip log distinguishes V2-delivered turns from attachment-only input. Deployments must always originate from GitHub: push the validated commit to the configured main branch and observe that exact GitHub commit in Railway. Never upload or redeploy through Railway CLI. Railway status/log inspection is read-only. This deployment preference was explicitly requested by the user.

Follow-up verification: 3709 tests in 292 files, typecheck with zero errors, 723 TypeScript sources and zero maintained JavaScript files. The rebuilt Node 22.23.3 container returns health 200 and exits 0 on SIGTERM (0.046 seconds). Evidence: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-production-continuation-budget-fix.json. Production behavior still requires a fresh user turn after the GitHub release.

### Public dialogue, bounded personal context and rejected transport recovery

The shared loop preserves assistant content in durable history and exposes only the last completed, non-tool public response as conversationReply. Productivity results and UI contracts carry this optional channel without invalidating old checkpoints. The WhatsApp presenter combines final dialogue with fully confirmed cards; failures, partial outcomes, unknown effects and loop notices retain host-owned wording. Intermediate tool-call prose is never rendered as public dialogue. Empty output reports an unavailable useful response instead of displaying a capability menu. Reply history remains available on subsequent turns.

The production composition retrieves at most two owned relevant memories and two standing preferences in parallel with subscription state. Each optional read has a 1500 ms ceiling, and one slow read does not erase another successful read. Name and content lengths are bounded, foreign records are excluded, and memory/preference tags escape delimiter characters. The concise executive-assistant and second-brain persona asks for natural, warm WhatsApp dialogue and verified confirmations. Optional personal context is removed before essential current protocol state when the complete native request exceeds its 1800-token limit. Successful reads retain scoped followup schemas without also retaining an unnecessary read schema. This block restores retrieval; durable memory learning and a foreground next-best-action port remain separate work.

A shared grapheme-aware boundary enforces 4000 UTF-16 units on presenter text and phone/business-recipient text payloads. Truncated presentation ends with an ellipsis while full results and source records remain durable. Unicode families and combining characters are never split by truncation. Interactive field-specific limits remain enforced by their existing adapters.

The two historical read-only Kapso rejections were resolved through service-only, security-invoker RPCs. The first source row holds a durable maintenance lease; owner/thread binding, exact expected source response, known read-only operation manifest, oversized original reply and the explicit HTTP 400/code 100 evidence are checked before each resolution. The lease-owning row is closed last. Original response fields and last_error are retained, and an agentCoreTransportRejected certificate records the source fingerprint and provider rejection. No message is replayed, no domain mutation is executed, and no delivery is fabricated. The reader skips only a matching completed resolution certificate; unknown or foreign evidence keeps its reconciliation fence. Receipt: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-leandryss-rejected-transport-resolution.json.

The repo now has syntax/safety lint and an offline durable-conversation integration configuration. TypeScript 7.0.2 remains the strict compiler; the documented TypeScript 6 compatibility API supplies ESLint's parser. SDK boundaries remain narrow structural interfaces with validated data, avoiding unrelated ambient browser declarations in the server compilation graph. CI runs both lint and conversational acceptance. Live deployment must originate from GitHub.

Final acceptance: 3715 unit tests across 293 files and seven offline integration journeys passed. Typecheck and lint report zero errors; lint covers 730 files and has zero warnings. Inventory: 730 TypeScript sources and zero maintained JavaScript. The discovery stage omits historical reference bodies and loads bounded reference hints when an atomic domain is selected, so a greeting can fit even with five long historical task titles. The regression failed before that change and now passes without deleting durable tasks. The rebuilt production container runs Node 22.23.3, returns health 200, exits 0 on SIGTERM, and matches 614 workspace runtime artifacts. Its compiled-router simulations deliver natural greetings and dialogue with a verified cancellation, with native input estimates no higher than 1569. Evidence: odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-conversation-restoration.json. The queue recovery is applied; application rollout is a separate GitHub release.


### V1 retirement: local time and verified execution dialogue

Phase 0 injects `userLocalTime` using the user's locale and timezone, adds local-time dialogue guidance, and skips empty gate/reconciled administrative closures. Phase 0 initially kept null/missing baseline responses non-transferable; the continuity reader described below now imports their bounded dialogue after checking for active/pending work. Prompt guidance reduces mistakes; it does not certify generated facts.

The next block introduces an optional typed dialogue discriminator. Conversation-only turns retain model text. Turns with domain outcomes use an execution channel with a finite, host-selected tone; the presenter owns acknowledgement and factual cards. Untrusted model drafts remain in history and `conversationReply` for provenance, but are never published alongside domain outcomes. This guard also applies to older payloads without the discriminator and prevents a forged conversation discriminator from bypassing the domain check. Dates, counts, resource names and URLs come from existing certified card rendering; they are not copied from the model's final paragraph. Failures, partial outcomes and provider notices retain honest status wording.

A supportive acknowledgement is selected only from explicit tiredness/long-day language in the current user input. It is not an additional inference request or an unrestricted generated preface. Arbitrary free conversation is not a factual verification channel, and semantic quality still needs bounded live evaluation.

Regression journeys inject wrong UTC/local times, wrong dates, invented bulk actions and unlimited inventory claims. They verify that the delivered text and durable delivery plan use the certified cards, that mutation dispatch happens once, and that raw draft provenance is preserved. CI now enforces `migration:check` instead of a vacuous JavaScript syntax scan in a TypeScript-only repository. It also builds the production image and runs `smoke:container`: the original compiled server must start on Node 22, return health 200, omit source/compiler/tsx, and exit cleanly on SIGTERM. External calls are empty fixtures in a container without network access; no paid inference or production writes occur. This is a build/runtime gate, not live model-quality certification.

The next retirement block must replace the productivity bridges to `executeTool` with direct typed domain-service ports. Existing services and background scheduling remain until their callers are accounted for; the V1 router and handoff cannot be removed before that parity and continuity gate. Roll back this dialogue change by reverting the dialogue schema/runner/presenter and corresponding tests together; phase 0 remains an independent commit.


### Productivity implementation relocation

Task, reminder and calendar operation implementations now live in `src/features/*/operations`. The 24 old tool entry points retain compatibility exports, so V1 callers and existing API tests keep the same functions while domain code has one implementation. SQL ownership, recurring-reminder cancellation, companion cleanup and Google authentication behavior are reused rather than reimplemented.

Reminder-prefix and phone normalization are pure shared helpers. Previously importing either helper through the WhatsApp client pulled the webhook, router-core and generic dispatcher into the domain graph. Transport and domain callers now share those helpers without that cycle. Existing task/reminder/calendar parity and calendar source-boundary checks are retained against the relocated implementations.


### Direct V2 productivity services

The canary composition now registers `createTaskDomainPort`, `createReminderDomainPort` and `createCalendarDomainPort`. Their default implementations call typed feature services with `TaskRequest`, `ReminderRequest` and `CalendarRequest`; no `manage_*` request or `executeTool` dispatcher participates in the production V2 domain path. Normalizers still validate returned identities, structured evidence, partial outcomes and operation receipts. Legacy ports retain their old executor injection/default for remaining callers and parity tests, but are outside the V2 dependency graph.

Before a native handler runs, its service binds the authenticated user and language/timezone snapshot to the current context, requires the durable inbound lease, checks run/request identity and asserts lease activity/cancellation. Registry capabilities, write authorization and journaling remain in the V2 loop. Unknown domain outcomes are never upgraded to success by a conflicting success/partial flag. Missing optional inputs are removed explicitly; a nullable calendar description maps to an empty description when clearing it. No service retries mutations.

`check:v2-boundary` parses compiled ESM with the TypeScript API and walks the V2 composition's local dependency graph. CI rejects generic tool dispatch, router-core, legacy domain adapters, missing/escaping local modules and unbounded dynamic imports. The reviewed module-level literal SDK import remains supported; shadowed/dynamic imports do not qualify. The gate checks 160 local modules, and its adversarial fixtures cover static/bare/re-export/lazy paths and invalid module boundaries.

Acceptance: 3761 unit tests across 295 files, 12 durable conversation integration tests, strict typecheck, lint and maintained-source inventory passed. The offline compiled Node 22 router performs a scoped read and one cancellation under a durable lease, sends once and stays within the 1800 input target; its maximum wire estimate is 1672. Container startup, health 200 and SIGTERM exit 0 are certified with synthetic dependencies and no network access. Evidence: `odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-direct-productivity-services.json`.

Retirement remains gradual: generic V1 dispatch still serves baseline callers; reusable target-resolution/presentation helpers are still under `src/agent/tools`, and non-productivity ingress/history compatibility remains to be accounted for. This block removes V1 execution from the production V2 productivity composition; it does not remove every legacy file or deploy a new application version. Rollback requires reverting direct service wiring before the implementation relocation commit.


### Reviewed V1 callers and conversation continuity

`config/v1-retirement-callers.json` records 13 runtime import edges into the canary entry, baseline engine, generic dispatcher and legacy active-run engine. `inventory:v1` parses TypeScript imports, exports and literal lazy imports in `src` and `scripts`, excludes type-only declarations and fails CI if the reviewed inventory drifts. This is a direct-edge inventory of named execution boundaries, not a claim that every shared legacy helper has been removed. `check:v2-boundary` independently walks the complete compiled V2 productivity dependency graph.

| Surface | Current execution and context | Retirement requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WhatsApp text and transcribed audio | Durable lease plus canary admission; V2 imports eligible completed baseline references through owner-filtered reads | Keep verified handoff until old histories are migrated; never retry V2 failures in V1 |
| Images, documents and location | Valid host-bound extraction excerpts/coordinates enter V2; caption-only grants and one committed write cap preserve the separation of evidence and intent | Add ranged/full-document retrieval, richer caption authorization and explicit bulk operations before claiming full attachment parity |
| Buttons, lists and interactive flows | Webhook paths invoke typed feature/database operations before the LLM router | Preserve lease, receipt and recipient checks; these flows are not disposable V1 reasoning code |
| User onboarding | Shared typed user feature called during user preparation | Preserve independent onboarding behavior |
| Personal memory | V2 reads personalization and exposes native memory_read/save/forget with explicit source, semantic admission and owned deletion receipts | Correction/versioning, implicit clarification lifecycle, broader semantic lookup and automatic extraction/learning remain before full parity |
| Subscriptions, projects, entities, admin and unified agenda | Generic V1 tools remain; V2 atomic registry covers tasks, reminders and calendar | Provide equivalent typed tools and authorization, or explicitly retire each capability |
| Billing | Stripe callbacks/checkout are independent of the reasoning engine | Keep Stripe; Lemon Squeezy and Mercado Pago remain retired |
| Approval and active-run continuation | Legacy execution still has durable claims/receipts; certified completed resolutions can enter V2 | Migrate active lifecycle explicitly; pending/uncertain runs are never replayed by handoff |
| CLI, developer chat and live evals | Router callers without an inbound lease still use baseline | Add a leased V2 harness before treating these evals as V2 evidence |
| Scheduler, morning briefing and Calendar watch | Independent typed jobs and shared domain services | Preserve delivery/scheduling invariants; do not remove jobs with the V1 router |

The compatibility reader never executes the old engine. It reads only an authenticated completed queue anchor, rejects pending clarification, active runs and unknown/partial effects, and refreshes owned task/reminder/calendar records. Null completed responses contribute no invented resource or execution receipts. Missing/unsupported references retain their original ordinal, title hint and source run/request as `legacyHandoff.referenceGaps`; they cannot authorize mutations. Valid references retain their original positions, so deleting a missing first entry never silently makes the previous second item become the first.

Only six dialogue messages and 2400 characters are imported, with an 800-character ceiling per message. The current inbound message is excluded by `messageId`, not text equality; identical older commands remain distinct. Stored history, queue responses and delivery evidence remain unchanged. V2 compaction and the 1800-token input ceiling remain authoritative. Historical hints are optional prompt projections and are dropped before essential tool protocol state when budget is tight; full provenance stays in the durable checkpoint.

Completed V2 productivity, active-completion and active-resolution checkpoints pass their existing semantic/receipt validators. Empty certified admission rejections and reconciled closures are skipped; an unknown mutation, attempted send or lost acceptance receipt still quarantines continuity, even beneath newer completed conversations. A failed/unverifiable handoff records a no-effect admission rejection and dispatches neither engine. No domain retry, historical send or administrative reset is performed by this reader.

CI runs the existing public conversation journeys plus `v2-legacy-continuity.integration.test.ts`. External storage, inference and transport use fixtures; the router, checkpoint reader, handoff, loop, journal, recovery and presenter execute their production code. Journeys cover old history and transcribed audio, null responses, safe rejection closures, missing references, completed continuation, lost write-outcome checkpoints and lost send receipts. Legacy protocol adapters in the test fixture simulate domain replies; the separate compiled V2 graph and container checks certify native production wiring. Offline passing tests do not certify live conversational quality or resolve existing production incidents.

Removal remains gated by attachment/domain parity and explicit pending-run migration. The existing 100-request checkpoint ledger bound remains unchanged; replacing it requires a durable archive/continuity design rather than silently discarding replay protection. This block prepares continuity and inventories the remaining work; it does not deploy or fully remove V1.


### Input-budget comparison before changing production

The production limit remains 1800, as requested. `eval:context-budgets` runs the same seven scripted fixtures at 1800 and 2500 using the core loop, full atomic registry, discovery, compact observations, 256-token continuation reserve and OpenRouter native input estimation. CI fails on exceeded input ceilings, extra mutations or failed expected completion. No provider, storage or transport call is made.

In this fixture set both limits finish the greeting, legacy-history greeting and read/cancel flows. The legacy-history read/cancel path peaks at 1759 estimated input tokens with 1800 and 2314 with 2500: the larger budget keeps more historical context. A synthetic 5000-character current input cannot start under 1800 but completes under 2500. This identifies a capacity boundary; it does not prove that a live model is more intelligent with 2500. Live comparison must measure correctness, conversational continuity, actual provider tokens, latency and cost with the same consented cases before choosing a new production limit. Evidence: `odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-context-budget-comparison.json`.


### Structured media ingress in V2

The webhook now passes `TurnMediaInput` separately from legacy model text. File inputs bind the authenticated user and inbound message ID to a surrogate-safe excerpt of at most 800 characters, an extraction-coverage marker and truncation status. Locations carry validated coordinates and a label capped at 120 characters; they do not set the user's timezone. Empty extractions are not promoted to file evidence. Invalid coordinates remain unsupported without throwing during input construction. Raw extraction/quoted text continues to be preserved by existing history persistence; V2 receives only the bounded structured projection.

Canary admission checks the user/request/modality binding and trusted delivery port before invoking V2. Invalid structured evidence records a certified no-effect rejection. Eligible old conversations use the verified handoff; existing V2 histories no longer reject a successfully extracted image/document or valid location solely because of its modality. Disabled canary and unsupported/unextracted media retain their pre-admission behavior. There is no V1 retry after V2 admission.

The caption supplies language detection, memory-search query and permitted tool operations. OCR, quoted transport messages and location labels never supply grants. Attachment-only turns expose no tools and use the natural conversation channel. Captions naming a domain may expose its read tool; explicit direct ES/EN instructions expose the requested write operation for one unambiguous domain. Quoted spans are excluded from grants while quoted resource titles remain usable as data. Negated commands, indirect instructions and ambiguous multiple domains require clarification or a subsequent explicit text request. This is a conservative ingress authorization boundary, not a general natural-language intent parser or complete caption parity.

Media turns can commit at most one successful/partial write. `maxWriteOperations` is a host option in the core loop; read operations and schema-error self-correction before the first write remain available. Additional writes receive `WRITE_OPERATION_LIMIT` before journaling dispatch. Existing non-media turns retain their previous write allowance. Durable receipts, owned-reference checks and lease-bound host authorization still apply. Dialogue tone reads the authored caption rather than OCR text. V2 uses its own transport result interface instead of the legacy router's response union.

The existing visual/PDF extractor remains upstream and may make its own configured provider request in production. The 1800-token ceiling applies to the V2 reasoning requests, not the bytes or tokens consumed by extraction. Partial extraction guidance forbids claiming full-file coverage; this block does not add binary multimodal reasoning, full-document search or retrieval of omitted content. These are explicit remaining media capabilities.

`v2-media-ingress.integration.test.ts` runs the actual webhook, canary router, V2 native adapters, loop, durable journal and presenter with mocked extraction/provider/domain services/transport. It covers natural file dialogue, old-history admission, coordinates, one verified caption mutation, denied OCR commands, prevention of extra writes and bounded extraction. Unit tests cover owner/message/modality mismatches, invalid coordinates, surrogate boundaries, caption negation/quoted text and write self-correction. CI includes these journeys in `test:conversation`, together with the existing continuity tests and unchanged 1800/2500 offline comparison.

Rollback: revert the structured media contracts/builder, webhook option, canary admission, V2 caption capability/prompt options, loop write cap and corresponding tests/docs as one work unit. Previous continuity and direct domain services remain independent. No production deployment or incident reconciliation is performed by this block. Next retirement work is memory mutation/extraction parity and the remaining non-productivity tools; unsupported media and approval lifecycles still need explicit coverage before removing V1 entirely.


### Explicit personal memory tools

V2 registers `memory_read`, `memory_save` and `memory_forget` directly through a native domain port. No generic V1 dispatcher or legacy memory tool participates. Conversations gain optional `memories` and `memoryReferenceOrder` fields; missing fields stay absent, preserving old checkpoint fingerprints and completion validators. Memory reference order is independent of later task/calendar reads and tracks the memory rows actually displayed.

Reads project six owned active fields, return at most five rows, and support escaped literal content search. Existing optional personalization continues using the shared relevance/standing-preference retrieval. Writes require the queue lease, matching user/run/request identities and host authorization. Save content must be an exact span in the current authored utterance; optional offsets identify the span, otherwise the host locates it. The selected schema's prompt includes `memorySourceText`, separate from OCR and transport quotes. Direct authored intent may follow a natural introductory clause. Semantic admission reuses the existing attribution/durability policy through the configured structured model gateway, rejecting mere mentions, quotation, negated attribution and transient operational state.

The native store derives an exact category/content identity and reuses an existing identical active row on sequential saves. It does not infer a canonical topic, replace other memories or invoke eager reminder-policy reconciliation. A same-topic correction therefore requires an explicit subsequent lifecycle operation; paraphrase equivalence and concurrent duplicate prevention are not certified here. Embeddings retain the current compatible 768-dimensional space. Optional embedding failure leaves a grounded stored fact with lexical retrieval; it does not authorize another storage attempt.

Forgetting requires an observed active UUID. The port refreshes the owned row and semantically checks that its whole fact lies within the authored deletion request, using the captured reference order. Scope ambiguity causes clarification before deletion. The final delete filters owner, UUID, active state, exact content and category, preventing a changed row from widening the earlier authorization. Confirmed SDK results become operation receipts; a lost write/deletion response remains indeterminate and blocks automatic replay. Read/admission failures before storage remain known no-write outcomes. No live writes or schema migrations were needed for implementation.

Certified memory cards use returned records rather than generated confirmation prose. When a read resolves a deletion, the read card omits the deleted ID; remaining visible ordinal references are updated. Forgotten IDs cannot authorize another deletion and are excluded from model references. Previous statements and escaped tool observations about forgotten facts are redacted in a model-only projection; fresh current user input may restate a fact. Durable conversation/operation evidence remains unchanged. This is profile-memory deletion with audit retention, not erasure of every conversation copy or inactive historical version.

Auxiliary admission and scope selection have an 1800 input ceiling, a 3000 total ceiling and 1024 maximum output tokens; model output needs room for reasoning before the small positions response. Gateway attempt/generation and usage logs carry the run/request and phase. Embedding accounting is separate. `turn_usage` explicitly labels its reasoning-loop scope; supplementary usage must be included when assessing end-to-end cost. Neither the offline fixtures nor the existing context-budget comparison measure live semantic quality, latency or actual spend.

Validation combines the actual V2 composition, native store query boundary, durable journal and presenter with deterministic model/storage/transport fixtures. Cases cover exact grounded save, optional-span resolution, idempotence, rejected semantic admission, lease loss, tenant isolation, read/forget in budget, refused deletion scope, aligned remaining references, lost receipts, future-turn quarantine and forgotten-fact redaction. The semantic adapter also runs through the real structured provider using a simulated HTTP response. Read-only Supabase checks confirmed the existing columns and the bounded query's `idx_memories_user_active` plan; a nil-user test query returned no rows. The maintained V1 caller inventory remains 13 edges.

Rollback this memory work unit across contracts/tools, native adapter/store, model projection/presentation and corresponding tests. Existing productivity services, media ingress and baseline compatibility remain independent. Next work must implement explicit corrections/versioning and typed clarification continuation, then automatic grounded learning, reminder-preference side-effect receipts and richer semantic/bulk/inactive-history operations. These limitations prevent declaring memory parity or complete V1 retirement yet. Evidence: `odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-explicit-memory-tools.json`.

### Proactive memory extraction after V2 delivery

V2 now schedules optional learning only after the durable assistant publishes completion and all response messages have accepted delivery receipts. The foreground response does not await extraction, admission, embeddings or storage. The webhook keeps its V2 exclusion to avoid a second legacy extraction job.

The native background adapter extracts at most three exact authored-source spans classified as stable personal facts or standing preferences. Semantic admission independently checks the full source before each save. Assistant replies, attachment-only observations, quoted payloads, fabricated spans and transient operational state are not factual authority. Explicit memory save/forget turns retain their own mutation pipeline and are excluded from automatic learning. A newer authored forget request cancels this process's older queued/in-flight learning for that user; already committed writes still require normal owned forgetting.

Jobs run outside the inbound lease with an independent 20-second cancellation signal. The process queue has a 32-job cap; source turns over 2,400 characters are skipped rather than interpreted from a potentially misleading excerpt. Extraction uses the model-agnostic configured memory provider, a 1,800-token input budget, 3,000-token total budget and at most 1,024 output tokens. Admission and embeddings retain their separate bounded adapters. Attempt/usage logs distinguish these auxiliary calls from the foreground loop's consumption.

Storage reuses the native owner-scoped exact-content identity and optional compatible embeddings, without calling legacy `saveMemory` or changing reminders implicitly. Newly saved preference rows are available through the existing standing-preference reader and bounded `readPersonalContext` on subsequent turns. Personal facts are available through relevant-memory retrieval. When preferences are present, the persona directs Hermes to use them for schedules, advance notice and routines, while preserving the current explicit request and host authorization.

This is best-effort process-local learning: queue saturation and process restart can discard pending work; there is no durable outbox, distributed cancellation, semantic supersession or automatic retry after uncertain storage outcomes. Preference adaptation is prompt guidance, not a certified additional action. Scripted tests certify lifecycle, persistence and future prompt injection; live extraction quality and latency are not measured. The main reasoning input cap remains 1,800 tokens. Evidence: `odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-proactive-memory.json`.

### Memory reliability follow-up

The first reliability work unit enforces explicit owned/live personal-context rows and separates standing profile facts from behavioral preferences. It adds guidance against treating assistant history or contact labels as verified personal identity. The observed incorrect legacy name was retired only under its exact owner/content/ID filters. See [memory reliability](memory-reliability.md) for evidence limitations and the source-attribution, atomic-revision, conflict-retrieval and durable-learning work units inspired by Engram and claude-mem. Those persistence changes remain proposed.

### Discovery-to-create continuation budget

Production turn `72fb643c-d2e2-42e1-aa51-cbe8abf63e6d` selected `reminder_create` but had no domain effects and ended with `limit_reached`. Its background learning completed with zero saved memories; that was independent of reminder execution. Context preparation could throw `ContextLimitError` on its first optional-personalization candidate, bypassing the intended retries without those extras. New-resource schemas also loaded unrelated historical resource references.

The runner now catches only `ContextLimitError` while optional extras remain, then retries without those extras. Essential current-turn user/assistant/tool pairs remain intact; other errors propagate. Under compact observation mode, new-resource tools omit historical ID reference arrays; existing-resource read/update/cancel/delete paths retain them. Durable records/history are unchanged, and the host still enforces the 1,800-token input estimate. Historical shorthand in a new request may still need clarification; this change does not interpret the competing durations in an audio transcript or certify live temporal reasoning.

Regression tests cover an observed-owned reminder ledger followed by discovery/create/verified delivery without `limit_reached`, and an optional-context budget exception falling back to the current user turn. Evidence: `odd/evidence/agent-core-phase5/v2-create-continuation-budget.json`.

### Current context policy and compound-request evaluation

The production composition now selects 2,500 input tokens per request with active-turn verified-batch compaction and bounded compound-action limits. See [V2 context budget](v2-context-budget.md) for the Pi/OpenCode/OpenAI comparison, the seven existing plus eighteen new offline fixtures, isolated live checks and their limits. Prior sections/evidence describing 1,800 remain historical; the shared `productivity-budget.ts` policy is authoritative for the current composition.
